<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a negative answer to Kirchberg's norm-ultrapower embedding problem. We construct an explicit separable unital full group <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra that admits no unital embedding into <i>B</i><sup><i>ω</i></sup>, for any nonzero unital nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra <i>B</i> and any free ultrafilter <i>ω</i> on ℕ. In particular, it does not embed unitally into $`\mathcal O_2^\omega`$.

- **C1** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $$G=\mathbb Z[1/2]^3\rtimes\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)\times\mathbb Z\bigr), \qquad (M,k)\cdot v=2^kMv,$$ and let $A=C^*(G)$ be its full group $C^*$-algebra. Then $A$ is separable and unital. For every nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and every free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there is no unital embedding $$A\longrightarrow B^\omega.$$ In particular, $A$ does not embed unitally into $\mathcal O_2^\omega$.
  - Explanation: The explicit full group algebra obstructs unital embeddings into every nuclear norm ultrapower under the stated hypotheses.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-group-data)): The group $\Gamma$ is finitely generated and has property (T). The map $\theta$ is injective, $$[\Gamma:\Lambda]=8, \qquad t\gamma t^{-1}=\theta(\gamma)\quad(\gamma\in\Gamma).$$ There is a nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representation $\pi_*$ of $\Gamma$ that factors through reduction modulo $2$ and is nontrivial on the translation subgroup $\mathbb Z^3$. In particular, $$\label{eq:group-aperiodic} \pi_*\not\simeq\pi_*\circ\theta^j\qquad(j\geq1).$$
  - Explanation: The base group has a finite-index self-embedding and an irreducible representation whose positive pullbacks cannot be equivalent to it.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-gap)): If $z_s=\sigma(s)$ for a unitary representation $\sigma:\Gamma\to\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(C)$ in a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}L(\mathbf z)\subseteq\{0\}\cup[\kappa,R].$$
  - Explanation: Exact unitary representations have a spectral gap above the invariant subspace.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-square-root-trace)): For positive Hilbert–Schmidt operators $X,Y$ on any Hilbert space, $$\\|X-Y\\|_2^2\leq\\|X^2-Y^2\\|_1.$$ Here $\\|\cdot\\|_1$ and $\\|\cdot\\|_2$ denote the trace and Hilbert–Schmidt norms, respectively.
  - Explanation: The trace inequality controls the Hilbert–Schmidt displacement of positive square roots.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound)): Let $\Gamma,S,\kappa$ satisfy <a href="#eq:kazhdan-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kazhdan-gap">[eq:kazhdan-gap]</a>. Let $\sigma:\Gamma\to\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation with no nonzero finite-dimensional subrepresentation. For any Hilbert space $E$ and any unitaries $(U_s)_{s\in S}$ on $E$, $$L\bigl((U_s\otimes\sigma(s))_{s\in S}\bigr)\geq b1, \qquad b=\frac{\kappa^2}{4\|S\|}.$$ No relations among the $U_s$ are required.
  - Explanation: Absence of finite-dimensional subrepresentations gives a uniform tensor-product lower bound even for unrelated first-factor unitaries.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness)): Let $\Gamma,S,\kappa,R,b,a$ be as above. For every nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and every unitary $S$-tuple $\mathbf u$ in $B$, the set $$D(\mathbf u)=\{[\pi]:d(\mathbf u,\pi)<a\}$$ is finite, where $\pi$ ranges over the nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations of $\Gamma$ and $[\pi]$ denotes its unitary equivalence class. No group relations on $\mathbf u$ are assumed.
  - Explanation: Nuclearity makes the set of approximate irreducible representation matches finite without imposing coordinate group relations.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-varying-gap)): For every sequence $(\pi_n)$ of nonzero finite-dimensional unitary representations of $\Gamma$, with no bound on their dimensions, $$\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)\in\{0\}\cup[\kappa,R]. \label{eq:varying-gap}$$ In particular, if $\{n:d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)<a\}\in\omega$, then this limit is zero.
  - Explanation: The ultralimit spectral dichotomy persists when the matrix dimensions vary without a bound.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-initial-match)): We have $\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_*)=0$, and consequently $\{n:[\pi_*]\in D_n\}\in\omega$.
  - Explanation: Injectivity of the assumed embedding places the distinguished representation in the matching sets on an ultrafilter-large set.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor)): The set $$\{n:(\forall[\pi]\in D_n)(\exists[\pi']\in D_n) \quad\pi\prec\pi'\circ\theta\}$$ belongs to $\omega$.
  - Explanation: Every member has an internal predecessor on the same ultrafilter-large set, rather than a separate set for each representation.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-predecessor-cycle)): Let $\theta:\Gamma\to\Gamma$ be a homomorphism, and let $D$ be a finite set of equivalence classes of nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations of $\Gamma$. Suppose that every $[\pi]\in D$ has $[\pi']\in D$ with $\pi\prec\pi'\circ\theta$. Then for every $[\pi]\in D$ there is an integer $p\geq1$ such that $\pi\simeq\pi\circ\theta^p$.
  - Explanation: A finite predecessor-closed set of irreducible classes forces periodicity under pullback.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-kep-consequences)): Neither $\mathcal O_2$ nor any exact or nuclear unital $C^*$-algebra is existentially closed among unital $C^*$-algebra extensions.* *For every free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there are a separable unital $C^*$-algebra $D$ and $\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(D)$ such that $D$ embeds unitally into $\mathcal O_2^\omega$ but $D\rtimes_\alpha\mathbb Z$ does not embed unitally there.* *Some satisfiable finite condition $p(x)=\{\varphi_j(x)<r_j:1\le j\le s\}$ in the unital $C^*$-algebra language, with $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_m)$, quantifier-free $\varphi_j$ and $r_j>0$, lacks good nuclear witnesses: for some $\varepsilon_0>0$, every realization $a=(a_i)_{i=1}^m$ of $p$ in every unital $C^*$-algebra $E$ satisfies $\max_{1\le i\le m}\\|\psi\phi(a_i)-a_i\\|\ge\varepsilon_0$ for every finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra $F$ and all completely positive contractive maps $\phi:E\to F$, $\psi:F\to E$, not necessarily unital.
  - Explanation: The negative embedding result entails failures of existential closure, crossed-product permanence and good nuclear witnesses; the latter witnesses are existential.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Norm ultrapower** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra $B$ and a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, its *norm ultrapower* is $$B^\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,B)/I_\omega(B),\qquad I_\omega(B)=\{(b_n):\lim_\omega\\|b_n\\|=0\}.$$ Here $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,B)$ is the algebra of bounded sequences, and the quotient uses the operator norm.
- **Cuntz algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Cuntz algebra $\mathcal O_2$ is the universal unital $C^*$-algebra generated by two isometries $s_1,s_2$ satisfying $s_1s_1^*+s_2s_2^*=1$; it was introduced by Cuntz (Cuntz 1977) [[Cuntz1977](#ref-Cuntz1977)].
- **Exactness** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here exactness means that minimal tensoring preserves short exact sequences.
- **Base subgroup** ([source](#sec:group)): Set $$\Gamma=\{(v,M,0):v\in\mathbb Z^3,\ M\in\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)\}, \qquad t=(0,I,1).$$
- **Scalar self-embedding** ([source](#sec:group)): We suppress the final coordinate when writing elements of $\Gamma$, and define $$\theta:\Gamma\longrightarrow\Gamma,\qquad \theta(v,M)=(2v,M), \qquad \Lambda=\theta(\Gamma).$$
- **Laplacian and spectral ceiling** ([source](#eq:kazhdan-gap)): For a tuple $\mathbf z=(z_s)_{s\in S}$ of unitaries in a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra $C$, set $$\label{eq:laplacian} L(\mathbf z)=\sum_{s\in S}(z_s-1)^*(z_s-1),\qquad R=4\|S\|.$$ Thus $0\le L(\mathbf z)\le R1$, without any group relations on $\mathbf z$.
- **Representation matching distance** ([source](#eq:matching-distance)): Define $$\label{eq:matching-distance} d(\mathbf z,\pi)= \min\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}L\bigl((z_s\otimes\bar\pi(s))_{s\in S}\bigr),$$ where the spectrum is taken in $C\otimes\mathcal B(\bar V_\pi)$.
- **Matching thresholds** ([source](#eq:block-minimum)): Fix once and for all $$\label{eq:thresholds} b=\frac{\kappa^2}{4\|S\|},\qquad 0<a<\min\{\kappa,b\}.$$
- **Approximate matching set** ([source](#stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness)): For every nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and every unitary $S$-tuple $\mathbf u$ in $B$, the set $$D(\mathbf u)=\{[\pi]:d(\mathbf u,\pi)<a\}$$ is finite, where $\pi$ ranges over the nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations of $\Gamma$ and $[\pi]$ denotes its unitary equivalence class.
- **Ultrafilter-large set** ([source](#sec:ultrapower-contradiction)): A set of indices is called $\omega$-large if it belongs to $\omega$.
- **Subrepresentation relation** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For representations $\alpha$ and $\beta$ of the same group, write $\alpha\prec\beta$ when $\alpha$ is unitarily equivalent to a subrepresentation of $\beta$.
- **Existential closure** ([source](#sec:consequences)): A unital algebra $E$ is *existentially closed* if, for every unital inclusion $E\subseteq F$, every finite parameter tuple $a$ from $E$, and every quantifier-free formula $\varphi(a,y)$ in a finite tuple $y$, $$\inf_{y\in E}\varphi(a,y)=\inf_{y\in F}\varphi(a,y),$$ where $y$ ranges over the corresponding products of prescribed norm balls.
- **Satisfiable finite condition** ([source](#sec:consequences)): A parameter-free finite condition consists of finitely many strict inequalities $\varphi_j(x)<r_j$ with quantifier-free $\varphi_j$. It is *satisfiable* when some tuple in a unital $C^*$-algebra satisfies them.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-group-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-group-data): Construct the scalar subgroup image and obtain an irreducible constituent of a finite regular representation. -> The base group has a finite-index self-embedding and an irreducible representation whose positive pullbacks cannot be equivalent to it.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-spectral-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spectral-gap): Split a faithful Hilbert-space realization into invariant vectors and their orthogonal complement. -> Exact unitary representations have a spectral gap above the invariant subspace.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-square-root-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-square-root-trace): Use the sign of the difference of positive operators and trace positivity. -> The trace inequality controls the Hilbert–Schmidt displacement of positive square roots.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound): Pass to the reduced density operator, apply the square-root trace inequality and the Kazhdan gap, then use Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Absence of finite-dimensional subrepresentations gives a uniform tensor-product lower bound even for unrelated first-factor unitaries.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness): Remove finite-dimensional parts in a matrix corona and transfer the tensor lower bound using nuclearity. -> Nuclearity makes the set of approximate irreducible representation matches finite without imposing coordinate group relations.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-varying-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-varying-gap): Form the varying-matrix norm ultraproduct and compute the minimum spectrum from the norm of the complementary positive operator. -> The ultralimit spectral dichotomy persists when the matrix dimensions vary without a bound.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-initial-match) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-match): Induce the distinguished representation to the ambient group and use spectrum preservation under the injective amplification. -> Injectivity of the assumed embedding places the distinguished representation in the matching sets on an ultrafilter-large set.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor): Transport approximate invariant vectors across conjugation and finite-index induction, then select an irreducible constituent. -> Every member has an internal predecessor on the same ultrafilter-large set, rather than a separate set for each representation.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-predecessor-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-predecessor-cycle): Follow predecessors to a repeated class, use dimension equality and propagate irreducibility back to the starting representation. -> A finite predecessor-closed set of irreducible classes forces periodicity under pullback.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:ultrapower-contradiction): Intersect the ultrafilter-large sets and contradict the translation behavior of the distinguished representation. -> The explicit full group algebra obstructs unital embeddings into every nuclear norm ultrapower under the stated hypotheses.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-cor-kep-consequences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-kep-consequences): Apply the stated equivalences for the embedding problem to obtain existential consequences. -> The negative embedding result entails failures of existential closure, crossed-product permanence and good nuclear witnesses; the latter witnesses are existential.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->
```json
{
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-embedding**: The specified full group algebra admits no unital embedding into a norm ultrapower of a nonzero unital nuclear algebra.
- **I-cosets**: Parity representatives give the left cosets of the doubled translation subgroup, with unique integral translation decomposition.
- **I-pullback**: Positive pullbacks make the translation action trivial after reduction modulo $2$, while the distinguished representation has nontrivial translation action.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-target**: Apply the obstruction to an arbitrary target algebra. -> The tensor-norm transfer has no stated justification without nuclearity.
- **AU-nonfinite**: Use nonfiniteness alone to exclude the embedding. -> The proposed target itself contains proper isometries.
- **AU-finite**: Treat a finite translation calculation as a decision procedure for norm-ultrapower embeddability. -> The finite calculation does not establish the nuclear finiteness or ultrafilter passages.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The integral coset decomposition and positive dyadic reduction are finite constructive parts of the group-data proof. This calculation supplies translation witnesses; it does not decide the operator-algebra embedding obstruction or construct the distinguished irreducible representation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 1000 cases {v: three integers, j: integer}.
# PRECONDITIONS: abs(v_i) <= 2**128 and 1 <= j <= 100; no booleans.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of {r, w, p}; v = r + 2*w, r in {0,1}^3, p = 2**j*v modulo 2.
# TIME: O(N*(B+j)) bit operations for bounded-size integer arithmetic.
# SPACE: O(N*(B+j)) bits including input and output, B the input bit length.
# ARITHMETIC: exact arbitrary-precision integers.
# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input raises an exception; no approximate result.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(cases, list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 1000
answer = []
for case in cases:
    assert isinstance(case, dict) and set(case) == {'v', 'j'}
    v, j = case['v'], case['j']
    assert isinstance(v, list) and len(v) == 3
    assert all(type(t) is int and abs(t) <= 2**128 for t in v)
    assert type(j) is int and 1 <= j <= 100
    z = [fmpz(t) for t in v]
    residue = [int(t % 2) for t in z]
    remainder = [int(t // 2) for t in z]
    pull = [int((fmpz(2)**j * t) % 2) for t in z]
    answer.append({'r': residue, 'w': remainder, 'p': pull})
print(json.dumps(answer, separators=(',', ':')))
```

```json
        "receipt_sha256": "986020393e6f7319b08dd251341f59983914ffffcaf2fa7338e61f83749930ba"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "Odd translations retain nonzero residue.",
        "id": "M-pullback",
        "mutation": "Omit the dyadic scaling before reduction.",
        "observed_exit": 7,
        "receipt_sha256": "ffeb157f374e91f1d46182d6c267fd8cf8ebaba774ae855b9fa8ab4f94a791c9"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import sys as system\nimport json as codec\nfrom itertools import product as cartesian\ntry:\n    inputs = codec.loads(open(system.argv[1]).read())\n    outputs = codec.loads(open(system.argv[2]).read())\n    declared = codec.loads(system.argv[3])\n    assert outputs == declared and len(outputs) == len(inputs)\n    for data, witness in zip(inputs, outputs):\n        assert set(witness) == {'r', 'w', 'p'}\n        assert all(isinstance(witness[key], list) and len(witness[key]) == 3 for key in witness)\n        assert all(type(number) is int for key in witness for number in witness[key])\n        matches = []\n        for representative in cartesian((0, 1), repeat=3):\n            differences = [original - bit for original, bit in zip(data['v'], representative)]\n            if not any(delta & 1 for delta in differences):\n                matches.append((list(representative), [delta >> 1 for delta in differences]))\n        assert len(matches) == 1\n        assert (witness['r'], witness['w']) == matches[0]\n        assert all(original == bit + (rest << 1) for original, bit, rest in zip(data['v'], witness['r'], witness['w']))\n        exact_pullback = [(original << data['j']) & 1 for original in data['v']]\n        assert witness['p'] == exact_pullback == [0, 0, 0]\nexcept Exception:\n    system.exit(7)\n",
    "output_contract": "Return the parity representative, integral remainder, and reduced positive dyadic pullback for each translation.",
    "reason": "The integral coset decomposition and positive dyadic reduction are finite constructive parts of the group-data proof. This calculation supplies translation witnesses; it does not decide the operator-algebra embedding obstruction or construct the distinguished irreducible representation.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 1000 cases {v: three integers, j: integer}.\n# PRECONDITIONS: abs(v_i) <= 2**128 and 1 <= j <= 100; no booleans.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of {r, w, p}; v = r + 2*w, r in {0,1}^3, p = 2**j*v modulo 2.\n# TIME: O(N*(B+j)) bit operations for bounded-size integer arithmetic.\n# SPACE: O(N*(B+j)) bits including input and output, B the input bit length.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact arbitrary-precision integers.\n# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input raises an exception; no approximate result.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert isinstance(cases, list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 1000\nanswer = []\nfor case in cases:\n    assert isinstance(case, dict) and set(case) == {'v', 'j'}\n    v, j = case['v'], case['j']\n    assert isinstance(v, list) and len(v) == 3\n    assert all(type(t) is int and abs(t) <= 2**128 for t in v)\n    assert type(j) is int and 1 <= j <= 100\n    z = [fmpz(t) for t in v]\n    residue = [int(t % 2) for t in z]\n    remainder = [int(t // 2) for t in z]\n    pull = [int((fmpz(2)**j * t) % 2) for t in z]\n    answer.append({'r': residue, 'w': remainder, 'p': pull})\nprint(json.dumps(answer, separators=(',', ':')))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An implementation contract for software is ABSENT. The result excludes a unital embedding of the specified full group algebra into a nuclear norm ultrapower. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game or simulation model is ABSENT. The group action defines the source algebra for an operator-algebra obstruction. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An executable tool contract is ABSENT. The proof establishes an algebraic obstruction rather than an effective embedding decision procedure. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An effective procedure to enumerate approximate representation matches is ABSENT. Finiteness of those matches in a nuclear algebra supplies a contradiction argument. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A computational representation of the finite matching sets is ABSENT. Their members are equivalence classes of irreducible unitary representations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A hardware execution model is ABSENT. The proof uses abstract operator algebras and Hilbert-space representations. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic construction or security assumption is ABSENT. The obstruction concerns embeddings of a full group algebra. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning model, training objective and statistical guarantee are ABSENT. Nuclearity is a tensor-norm hypothesis on the target algebra. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A finite optimization input contract is ABSENT. The spectral minimum is used in an abstract representation-matching argument. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed-system protocol is ABSENT. The norm ultrapower is a quotient of bounded algebra-valued sequences. |
| other (operator algebras and unitary representation theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The explicit full group algebra supplies an obstruction to unital embedding into norm ultrapowers of nonzero unital nuclear algebras. This is a mathematical applicability test on a proposed embedding; an executable decision procedure is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The target must be a nonzero unital nuclear algebra, and the ultrapower must use a free ultrafilter. The source is the specified full group algebra. Blocked use: ['Extending the obstruction to arbitrary source algebras or to targets whose nuclearity is unproved.']
- **L2** (counterexample_scope): The problem concerns norm ultrapowers and is distinguished from the tensor-norm formulation of Kirchberg’s conjecture and from Connes’ embedding problem. Blocked use: ['Claiming an equivalence with either of those distinct problems.']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): Nonfiniteness of the full group algebra alone does not supply the claimed obstruction, because the target can contain proper isometries. Blocked use: ['Using a proper isometry alone to rule out the embedding.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): Nuclearity is used to identify the minimal and maximal tensor products before transferring the lower bound. The coordinate quotient is not identified with the minimal tensor product. Blocked use: ['Dropping nuclearity from the finiteness argument.', 'Assuming an identification with the coordinate quotient.']
- **L5** (approximation): Coordinate lifts are unitary tuples whose multiplication defects vanish along the ultrafilter; they need not obey group relations at an individual coordinate. Blocked use: ['Applying the exact representation spectral gap directly to an arbitrary coordinate tuple.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The uniform lower bound requires that the second representation have no nonzero finite-dimensional subrepresentation; the first tuple needs no relations. Blocked use: ['Applying this lower bound when the second representation has a finite-dimensional constituent.']
- **L7** (approximation): The matching threshold is strictly below both the Kazhdan gap and the uniform lower bound. Blocked use: ['Replacing the strict matching cutoff with an unproved boundary rule.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): Finiteness of the representation-matching set does not provide an effective enumeration or a computable cardinality bound in the paper. Blocked use: ['Claiming an executable representation-matching algorithm from finiteness alone.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The predecessor argument needs a simultaneous predecessor for every member of the same coordinate matching set on a common ultrafilter-large set. Blocked use: ['Substituting separate index sets for each representation without the uniform quantifier argument.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The finite-chain conclusion concerns irreducible finite-dimensional representations and containment under pullback. Repetition in an arbitrary directed graph does not establish this representation-theoretic periodicity. Blocked use: ['Using graph repetition alone as a certificate for pullback periodicity.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The crossed-product and finite-condition witnesses are existential and are not identified by the explicit group construction. Blocked use: ['Publishing explicit witnesses for those corollary assertions without an additional construction.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): Finite arithmetic checks of the coset construction do not certify the analytic norm-ultrapower obstruction. A computational certificate of the complete theorem is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite coset test as verification of the full nonembedding theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 292 (`lean/docs/292.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An explicit obstruction to nuclear norm-ultrapower embeddings](../../preprints/An-explicit-obstruction-to-nuclear-norm-ultrapower-embeddings-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Kirchberg's norm-ultrapower embedding problem asks whether separable $C^*$-algebras embed into norm ultrapowers of nuclear algebras. The formalization constructs an explicit separable unital full group $C^*$-algebra that has no unital embedding into $B^\omega$ for any nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and any free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. In particular, it does not embed unitally into an ultrapower of the Cuntz algebra $\mathcal O_2$.

Comparator source `NuclearUltrapower.lean` (9376 bytes, sha256 `8b9f2669322ca4de1a9977e701fc42fc0248511ad0a2998540da0c971d6d2107`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
universe u v w
open Filter
open scoped TensorProduct Topology
namespace NuclearUltrapower

/- The dyadic coefficient ring is exactly Z[1/2]. -/
abbrev Dyadic := Localization.Away (2 : ℤ)
abbrev Vector3 := Fin 3 → Dyadic
abbrev SL3Z := Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup (Fin 3) ℤ

noncomputable def dyadicTwo : Dyadicˣ :=
  (IsLocalization.Away.isUnit_of_dvd (S := Dyadic) (2 : ℤ) (dvd_refl _)).unit

noncomputable def matrixAction (M : SL3Z) : Vector3 ≃ₗ[Dyadic] Vector3 :=
  Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup.toLin'
    (Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup.map (Int.castRingHom Dyadic) M)

noncomputable def dilationAction (g : SL3Z × Multiplicative ℤ) :
    Vector3 ≃ₗ[Dyadic] Vector3 :=
  (matrixAction g.1).trans (LinearEquiv.smulOfUnit (dyadicTwo ^ g.2.toAdd))

noncomputable def groupAction :
    (SL3Z × Multiplicative ℤ) →* MulAut (Multiplicative Vector3) where
  toFun g := AddEquiv.toMultiplicative (dilationAction g).toAddEquiv
  map_one' := by
    ext v i
    simp [dilationAction, matrixAction, LinearEquiv.smulOfUnit]
  map_mul' g h := by
    ext v i
    simp [dilationAction, matrixAction, LinearEquiv.smulOfUnit, zpow_add,
      map_mul, mul_smul, Units.smul_def, mul_left_comm]

/-- The exact group of the semidirect-product construction. -/
abbrev G := SemidirectProduct (Multiplicative Vector3)
  (SL3Z × Multiplicative ℤ) groupAction

section UltrapowerDefinition
variable (B : Type u) [CStarAlgebra B] (ω : Ultrafilter ℕ)

/-- Bounded sequences, with their supremum norm. N is discrete. -/
abbrev BoundedSeq := BoundedContinuousFunction ℕ B

/-- Equality modulo I_omega(B). This is equivalent to norm-null difference. -/
def ultraRel (x y : BoundedSeq B) : Prop :=
  Tendsto (fun n => x n - y n) (ω : Filter ℕ) (𝓝 0)

/-- The ring congruence defining the norm (not algebraic) ultrapower. -/
def ultraCon : RingCon (BoundedSeq B) where
  r := ultraRel B ω
  iseqv := {
    refl := fun x => by simp [ultraRel]
    symm := fun {x y} h => by
      simpa only [ultraRel, neg_sub, neg_zero] using h.neg
    trans := fun {x y z} hxy hyz => by
      simpa only [ultraRel, sub_add_sub_cancel, zero_add] using hxy.add hyz }
  add' := by
    intro x y z t hxy hzt
    simpa only [ultraRel, BoundedContinuousFunction.add_apply, ← add_sub_add_comm, zero_add]
      using hxy.add hzt
  mul' := by
    intro x y z t hxy hzt
    have h₁ := hxy.zero_mul_isBoundedUnder_le (Filter.isBoundedUnder_of ⟨‖z‖, fun index => z.norm_coe_le_norm index⟩)
    have h₂ := Filter.isBoundedUnder_le_mul_tendsto_zero (Filter.isBoundedUnder_of ⟨‖y‖, fun index => y.norm_coe_le_norm index⟩) hzt
    simpa only [ultraRel, BoundedContinuousFunction.mul_apply, sub_mul, mul_sub,
      sub_add_sub_cancel, zero_add] using h₁.add h₂

/-- The exact quotient l-infinity(N,B)/I_omega(B) as a complex star algebra.
The quotient identifies sequences whose difference tends to zero along the ultrafilter. -/
abbrev NormUltrapower := (ultraCon B ω).Quotient

instance : Star (NormUltrapower B ω) where
  star := Quotient.map' star (fun first second hypothesis => by
    change ultraRel B ω (star first) (star second)
    simpa only [ultraRel, star_sub, star_zero, BoundedContinuousFunction.star_apply] using hypothesis.star)

instance : StarRing (NormUltrapower B ω) where
  star_involutive := by
    intro x
    induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with | h x =>
      exact congrArg Quotient.mk'' (star_star x)
  star_mul := by
    intro x y
    induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with | h x =>
      induction y using Quotient.inductionOn with | h y =>
        exact congrArg Quotient.mk'' (star_mul x y)
  star_add := by
    intro x y
    induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with | h x =>
      induction y using Quotient.inductionOn with | h y =>
        exact congrArg Quotient.mk'' (star_add x y)

instance : StarModule ℂ (NormUltrapower B ω) where
  star_smul := by
    intro c x
    induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with | h x =>
      exact congrArg Quotient.mk'' (star_smul c x)

/-- Freeness: no finite subset of N belongs to the ultrafilter. -/
def IsFree (ω : Ultrafilter ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ F : Finset ℕ, (F : Set ℕ) ∉ (ω : Filter ℕ)

end UltrapowerDefinition

/-- A bundled complex Hilbert space, without any separability assumption. -/
structure ComplexHilbert where
  carrier : Type u
  [normed : NormedAddCommGroup carrier]
  [inner : InnerProductSpace ℂ carrier]
  [complete : CompleteSpace carrier]

attribute [instance] ComplexHilbert.normed ComplexHilbert.inner ComplexHilbert.complete
instance : CoeSort ComplexHilbert (Type u) := ⟨ComplexHilbert.carrier⟩

section TensorNormDefinitions
variable {B : Type u} {C : Type v} [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra B] [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra C]

/-- Spatial evaluation of the algebraic tensor product on separate Hilbert spaces.
Its operator norm is unchanged on the Hilbert completion. -/
def spatialEval (H K : ComplexHilbert.{w})
    (ρ : B →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (σ : C →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (K →L[ℂ] K)) :
    B ⊗[ℂ] C →ₗ[ℂ] ((H ⊗[ℂ] K) →L[ℂ] (H ⊗[ℂ] K)) :=
  TensorProduct.lift (LinearMap.mk₂ ℂ (fun b c => TensorProduct.mapL (ρ b) (σ c))
    (by intros; simp [TensorProduct.mapL_add_left])
    (by intros; simp [TensorProduct.mapL_smul_left])
    (by intros; simp [TensorProduct.mapL_add_right])
    (by intros; simp [TensorProduct.mapL_smul_right]))

/-- Evaluation on one Hilbert space; used only when the two ranges commute. -/
def commutingEval (H : ComplexHilbert.{w})
    (ρ : B →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (σ : C →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) :
    B ⊗[ℂ] C →ₗ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H) :=
  (LinearMap.mul' ℂ (H →L[ℂ] H)).comp
    (TensorProduct.map
      (show B →ₗ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H) from
        { toFun := ρ, map_add' := map_add ρ, map_smul' := map_smul ρ })
      (show C →ₗ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H) from
        { toFun := σ, map_add' := map_add σ, map_smul' := map_smul σ }))

/-- The minimal C*-tensor norm: supremum over spatial representations. -/
def minTensorNorm (x : B ⊗[ℂ] C) : ℝ :=
  sSup {r | ∃ (H K : ComplexHilbert.{w})
    (ρ : B →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (σ : C →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (K →L[ℂ] K)),
    r = ‖spatialEval H K ρ σ x‖}

/-- The maximal C*-tensor norm: supremum over commuting representations. -/
def maxTensorNorm (x : B ⊗[ℂ] C) : ℝ :=
  sSup {r | ∃ (H : ComplexHilbert.{w})
    (ρ : B →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)) (σ : C →⋆ₙₐ[ℂ] (H →L[ℂ] H)),
    (∀ b c, Commute (ρ b) (σ c)) ∧ r = ‖commutingEval H ρ σ x‖}

/-- Nuclearity in the tensor-norm convention of the tensor-norm formulation.
The second C*-algebra need not be unital, nonzero, or separable. -/
def IsNuclear (B : Type u) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra B] : Prop :=
  ∀ (C : Type v) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra C] (x : B ⊗[ℂ] C),
    minTensorNorm.{u, v, max u v} x = maxTensorNorm.{u, v, max u v} x
end TensorNormDefinitions

/-- A nonzero unital complex C*-algebra. -/
structure UnitalCStar where
  carrier : Type u
  [cstar : CStarAlgebra carrier]
  [nontrivial : Nontrivial carrier]
attribute [instance] UnitalCStar.cstar UnitalCStar.nontrivial
instance : CoeSort UnitalCStar (Type u) := ⟨UnitalCStar.carrier⟩

/-- The universal property defining the full, not the reduced, group C*-algebra.
The dense generation clause makes the intended completion explicit.
This is definition data, not a hypothesis silently supplied to the main claim. -/
def IsFullGroupAlgebra (A : UnitalCStar.{u}) (ι : G →* unitary A) : Prop :=
  Dense (Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range (fun g => (ι g : A))) : Set A) ∧
  ∀ (D : UnitalCStar.{u}) (ρ : G →* unitary D),
    ∃! φ : A →⋆ₐ[ℂ] D, ∀ g, φ (ι g) = (ρ g : D)

/-- The two Cuntz relations, including unit preservation explicitly. -/
def CuntzTwoRelations {D : Type u} [CStarAlgebra D] (s : Fin 2 → D) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, star (s i) * s i = 1) ∧ ∑ i, s i * star (s i) = 1

/-- The universal nonzero unital C*-algebra on the two Cuntz isometries. -/
def IsCuntzTwoAlgebra (O : UnitalCStar.{u}) (s : Fin 2 → O) : Prop :=
  CuntzTwoRelations s ∧
  ∀ (D : UnitalCStar.{u}) (r : Fin 2 → D), CuntzTwoRelations r →
    ∃! φ : O →⋆ₐ[ℂ] D, ∀ i, φ (s i) = r i

/-- The manuscript's main statement including its explicitly stated O_2
corollary. Both universal C*-algebras must be constructed to prove this. -/
def MainStatementWithCuntzCorollary : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : UnitalCStar.{u}) (ι : G →* unitary A),
    IsFullGroupAlgebra A ι ∧ TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace A ∧
    (∀ (B : UnitalCStar.{u}), IsNuclear.{u, u} B →
      ∀ (ω : Ultrafilter ℕ), IsFree ω →
        ¬ ∃ φ : A →⋆ₐ[ℂ] NormUltrapower B ω, Function.Injective φ) ∧
    ∃ (O : UnitalCStar.{u}) (s : Fin 2 → O), IsCuntzTwoAlgebra O s ∧
      ∀ (ω : Ultrafilter ℕ), IsFree ω →
        ¬ ∃ φ : A →⋆ₐ[ℂ] NormUltrapower O ω, Function.Injective φ

end NuclearUltrapower
end

namespace NuclearUltrapower

/-- The full dyadic group C*-algebra has no nuclear norm-ultrapower embedding,
including the corollary for the universal Cuntz algebra on two isometries. -/
theorem main_no_embedding : MainStatementWithCuntzCorollary.{0} := by
  sorry

end NuclearUltrapower

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a negative answer to Kirchberg's norm-ultrapower embedding problem. We construct an explicit separable unital full group <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra that admits no unital embedding into <i>B</i><sup><i>ω</i></sup>, for any nonzero unital nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra <i>B</i> and any free ultrafilter <i>ω</i> on ℕ. In particular, it does not embed unitally into $`\mathcal O_2^\omega`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### The embedding problem

Throughout, $C^*$-algebras are complex, and an *embedding* is an injective $*$-homomorphism. We state unit preservation explicitly. For a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra $B$ and a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, its *norm ultrapower* is $$B^\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,B)/I_\omega(B),\qquad
 I_\omega(B)=\{(b_n):\lim_\omega\|b_n\|=0\}.$$ Here $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,B)$ is the algebra of bounded sequences, and the quotient uses the operator norm. The Cuntz algebra $\mathcal O_2$ is the universal unital $C^*$-algebra generated by two isometries $s_1,s_2$ satisfying $s_1s_1^*+s_2s_2^*=1$; it was introduced by Cuntz (Cuntz 1977) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Cuntz1977](#ref-Cuntz1977)].

The embedding theorem of Kirchberg–Phillips (Kirchberg and Phillips 2000) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KirchbergPhillips2000](#ref-KirchbergPhillips2000)] states that every separable unital exact $C^*$-algebra embeds unitally into $\mathcal O_2$. Here exactness means that minimal tensoring preserves short exact sequences. Passing from $\mathcal O_2$ to its norm ultrapower asks how far this universality extends beyond exact algebras. The techniques behind the embedding theorem led Kirchberg to consider this possibility, as recounted in (Goldbring and Sinclair 2015, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[GoldbringSinclair2015](#ref-GoldbringSinclair2015)].

Kirchberg’s embedding problem asks whether every separable unital $C^*$-algebra admits a unital embedding into $\mathcal O_2^\omega$; see Goldbring–Sinclair (Goldbring and Sinclair 2015, sec. 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GoldbringSinclair2015](#ref-GoldbringSinclair2015)]. We give an explicit counterexample. The unital formulation is equivalent to the formulation without unit preservation (Goldbring and Sinclair 2015, sec. 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GoldbringSinclair2015](#ref-GoldbringSinclair2015)]. This question concerns norm ultrapowers of a nuclear $C^*$-algebra. It is distinct from the tensor-norm formulation of Kirchberg’s conjecture and from Connes’ embedding problem, as emphasized in (Goldbring and Sinclair 2015, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GoldbringSinclair2015](#ref-GoldbringSinclair2015)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $$G=\mathbb Z[1/2]^3\rtimes\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)\times\mathbb Z\bigr),
 \qquad (M,k)\cdot v=2^kMv,$$ and let $A=C^*(G)$ be its full group $C^*$-algebra. Then $A$ is separable and unital. For every nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and every free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there is no unital embedding $$A\longrightarrow B^\omega.$$ In particular, $A$ does not embed unitally into $\mathcal O_2^\omega$.*

</div>

The group is countable and independent of both $B$ and $\omega$; no separability assumption on $B$ is needed. The proof treats an arbitrary fixed nuclear $B$ throughout. Specializing to the nuclear algebra $\mathcal O_2$ gives the negative answer to the embedding problem; for its nuclearity, see (Paulsen and Zheng 2016, sec. 4, pp. 80–82) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[PaulsenZheng2016](#ref-PaulsenZheng2016)]. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">6</a> gives the consequences for existential closure, permanence of $\mathcal O_2^\omega$-embeddability under crossed products, and good nuclear witnesses, with the relevant notions defined there.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Previous work and the new obstruction

Recent approaches study computability and tensor norms. Fox–Goldbring–Hart (Fox, Goldbring, and Hart 2024) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FoxGoldbringHart2024](#ref-FoxGoldbringHart2024)] derive computability-theoretic consequences of a positive answer, using the computable presentation of $\mathcal O_2$. Goldbring–Sinclair give a conditional route to a negative answer through a *weakly explicit minimal tensor norm*: an effective choice of universal formulas approximating the minimal tensor-norm predicates to a fixed accuracy. They leave this property of $\mathcal O_2$ open (Goldbring and Sinclair 2025, Definition 4.16, Theorem 4.18 and Question 4.20) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GoldbringSinclair2025Tensor](#ref-GoldbringSinclair2025Tensor)]. Our obstruction instead comes from the representation theory of an explicit discrete group.

The group configuration already appears in the nonfiniteness arguments of Sauers (Sauers 2026, sec. 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Sauers2026NonMF](#ref-Sauers2026NonMF)] and Eckhardt (Eckhardt 2026, Theorem 3.1 and Example 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Eckhardt2026](#ref-Eckhardt2026)]. They show that the full algebra of the ascending HNN extension $G$ is nonfinite: it contains a proper isometry $v$, so $v^*v=1$ but $vv^*\ne1$. Its property (T) base group $\Gamma=\mathbb Z^3\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$ and its finite-index scalar self-embeddings are recorded in de Cornulier (Cornulier 2007, Proposition 1.1 and its proof) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[deCornulier2007](#ref-deCornulier2007)]. Sauers and Eckhardt also construct non-MF groups: groups admitting no injective homomorphism into the unitary group of any norm matrix corona $\prod_j M_{d_j}/\bigoplus_j M_{d_j}$, with positive integer dimensions $d_j$. Here the product consists of bounded sequences and the direct sum is the ideal of sequences tending to zero in norm (Eckhardt 2026, Theorem 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Eckhardt2026](#ref-Eckhardt2026)] <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Sauers2026NonMF](#ref-Sauers2026NonMF)](Sauers 2026, secs. 3–5). Eckhardt’s example is an associated wreath product. The target class here is different: nonfiniteness of $C^*(G)$ alone cannot exclude a unital embedding into $\mathcal O_2^\omega$, which itself contains proper isometries.

The additional ingredient is a finiteness statement for approximate finite-dimensional representation types inside a nuclear algebra (Lemma <a href="#lem:nuclear-finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:nuclear-finiteness">6</a>). Its tensor-product argument follows Wassermann’s method (Wassermann 1991) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Wassermann1991](#ref-Wassermann1991)], as explained by Manuilov–Thomsen (Manuilov and Thomsen 2007, sec. 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[ManuilovThomsen2007](#ref-ManuilovThomsen2007)]. The statement applies to an arbitrary tuple of unitaries, even when the tuple satisfies no group relations. This flexibility is essential because coordinate lifts of an ultrapower representation are only approximately multiplicative. A dimension-independent estimate allows the representation type to vary with the coordinate. Finite-index induction then relates members of the resulting finite sets by composition with the self-embedding. These two passages turn the group configuration into the norm-ultrapower obstruction.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof outline

The subgroup $\Gamma=\mathbb Z^3\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$ of $G$ has property (T). Conjugation by the generator $t$ of the last $\mathbb Z$-factor sends $\Gamma$ onto the index-eight subgroup $\theta(\Gamma)$, where $\theta(v,M)=(2v,M)$. There is a finite-dimensional irreducible representation $\pi_*$ of $\Gamma$ such that $\pi_*\circ\theta^j$ is inequivalent to $\pi_*$ for every $j\ge1$. Section <a href="#sec:group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:group">2</a> verifies these facts.

Fix a finite generating set $S$ of $\Gamma$ for which property (T) gives a spectral gap. Suppose a unital embedding $A\to B^\omega$ exists, and choose unitary lifts $x_n(g)\in B$ of its group unitaries. For each $n$, consider the nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations $\pi$ for which the tuple $(x_n(s)\otimes\bar\pi(s))_{s\in S}$ has spectral minimum below a fixed small threshold for the sum of squared displacements from $1$. Here $\bar\pi$ is the conjugate representation. Sections <a href="#sec:spectral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spectral">3</a> and <a href="#sec:nuclear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nuclear">4</a> specify the threshold and show that the resulting set $D_n$ of representation classes is finite.

Write $\pi\prec\rho$ when $\pi$ occurs as a unitary subrepresentation of $\rho$, and call $\pi'$ a predecessor of $\pi$ if $\pi\prec\pi'\circ\theta$. Faithfulness of the embedding places $[\pi_*]$ in $D_n$ on a set belonging to $\omega$. In Section <a href="#sec:contradiction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:contradiction">[sec:contradiction]</a>, the spectral gap for *every sequence* of finite-dimensional representations supplies, on one set belonging to $\omega$, the simultaneous implication $$[\pi]\in D_n\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \text{some }[\pi']\in D_n\text{ satisfies }\pi\prec\pi'\circ\theta.$$ Starting at $\pi_*$ and repeatedly choosing a predecessor must give a repetition in the finite set $D_n$. Irreducibility then makes $\pi_*$ periodic under composition with $\theta$, a contradiction.

<a id="sec:group"></a>
### The group and a finite-dimensional representation

We construct a finite-dimensional representation inequivalent to each of its positive pullbacks along a fixed group endomorphism. The same group configuration appears in the nonfiniteness arguments of Sauers (Sauers 2026, sec. 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Sauers2026NonMF](#ref-Sauers2026NonMF)] and Eckhardt (Eckhardt 2026, Theorem 3.1 and Example 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Eckhardt2026](#ref-Eckhardt2026)]. De Cornulier (Cornulier 2007, Proposition 1.1 and its proof) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[deCornulier2007](#ref-deCornulier2007)] records the property (T) affine group and its finite-index scalar self-embeddings. We use the dyadic case.

We use column vectors. In the group $G$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, write elements as $(v,M,k)$, so that $$(v,M,k)(w,N,\ell)=(v+2^kMw,MN,k+\ell).$$ Set $$\Gamma=\{(v,M,0):v\in\mathbb Z^3,\ M\in\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)\},
 \qquad t=(0,I,1).$$ We suppress the final coordinate when writing elements of $\Gamma$, and define $$\theta:\Gamma\longrightarrow\Gamma,\qquad
 \theta(v,M)=(2v,M),
 \qquad \Lambda=\theta(\Gamma).$$

<a id="stmt-lem-group-data"></a>
<div id="lem:group-data" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:group-aperiodic"></a>
**Lemma 2**. *The group $\Gamma$ is finitely generated and has property (T). The map $\theta$ is injective, $$[\Gamma:\Lambda]=8,
 \qquad t\gamma t^{-1}=\theta(\gamma)\quad(\gamma\in\Gamma).$$ There is a nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representation $\pi_*$ of $\Gamma$ that factors through reduction modulo $2$ and is nontrivial on the translation subgroup $\mathbb Z^3$. In particular, $$\label{eq:group-aperiodic}
 \pi_*\not\simeq\pi_*\circ\theta^j\qquad(j\geq1).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-group-data"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-group-data</summary>

*Proof.* Property (T) is the case $n=3$, $m=1$ of (Cornulier 2007, Proposition 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[deCornulier2007](#ref-deCornulier2007)], with $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$ acting on one copy of its standard rank-three integral module. For finite generation, take the three standard translations and the elementary matrices $I+E_{ij}$, $i\ne j$, where $E_{ij}$ is the matrix unit. These matrices generate $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$ by integer row reduction.

The group law shows that $\theta$ is an injective homomorphism and that conjugation by $t$ implements it. Its image is $\Lambda=2\mathbb Z^3\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z)$. The elements $$r_a=(a,I),\qquad a\in\{0,1\}^3,$$ are representatives of its eight left cosets: writing $v=a+2w$ gives $(v,M)=r_a(2w,M)$, and two such representatives give the same coset only when they have the same residue modulo $2$.

Let $F$ be the finite image of the reduction homomorphism $$\Gamma\longrightarrow (\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^3\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_3(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z),
 \qquad (v,M)\longmapsto(v\bmod2,M\bmod2).$$ The translation subgroup $(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^3$ acts nontrivially in the left regular representation of $F$. Decomposing this representation into irreducibles therefore gives an irreducible constituent that is nontrivial on translations. Its pullback to $\Gamma$ is the required $\pi_*$. Indeed, $\pi_*$ factors through reduction modulo $2$, whereas $\theta^j(v,I)=(2^jv,I)$. Thus $\pi_*\circ\theta^j$ is trivial on all translations for every $j\geq1$, and $\pi_*$ is not. This proves <a href="#eq:group-aperiodic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:group-aperiodic">[eq:group-aperiodic]</a>. ◻

</details>

The group $G$ is countable, so its full group $C^*$-algebra $C^*(G)$ is separable and unital. Every unitary representation of $G$ extends to $C^*(G)$. We will use this universal property after inducing $\pi_*$ from $\Gamma$ to $G$.

<a id="sec:spectral"></a>
### A spectral test for representation matching

<a id="eq:kazhdan-gap"></a>
We now introduce the spectral quantity used to form the finite sets in the proof outline. The discussion applies to any finitely generated discrete group $\Gamma$ with property (T). Choose a nonempty finite generating set $S$ containing a Kazhdan set. By property (T), after decreasing the constant if necessary, there is $0<\kappa\le4|S|$ such that every unitary representation $\sigma$ with no nonzero invariant vector satisfies $$\label{eq:kazhdan-gap}
 \sum_{s\in S}\|\sigma(s)\xi-\xi\|^2\ge\kappa\|\xi\|^2.$$ For a tuple $\mathbf z=(z_s)_{s\in S}$ of unitaries in a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra $C$, set <a id="eq:laplacian"></a>$$\label{eq:laplacian}
 L(\mathbf z)=\sum_{s\in S}(z_s-1)^*(z_s-1),\qquad R=4|S|.$$ Thus $0\le L(\mathbf z)\le R1$, without any group relations on $\mathbf z$.

<a id="stmt-lem-spectral-gap"></a>
<div id="lem:spectral-gap" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *If $z_s=\sigma(s)$ for a unitary representation $\sigma:\Gamma\to\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(C)$ in a nonzero unital $C^*$-algebra, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}L(\mathbf z)\subseteq\{0\}\cup[\kappa,R].$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-spectral-gap"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-spectral-gap</summary>

*Proof.* Represent $C$ faithfully and unitally on a Hilbert space. The invariant vectors for $\sigma$ and their orthogonal complement are reducing subspaces. On the former $L$ vanishes; on the latter <a href="#eq:kazhdan-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kazhdan-gap">[eq:kazhdan-gap]</a> gives $L\ge\kappa1$. A faithful unital representation preserves spectra, proving the claim. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:matching-distance"></a>
Every finite-dimensional representation below acts on a nonzero Hilbert space. If $\pi:\Gamma\to\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(V_\pi)$ is such a representation, its conjugate $\bar\pi$ acts on the conjugate Hilbert space $\bar V_\pi$ by $\bar\pi(g)\bar v=\overline{\pi(g)v}$. Define $$\label{eq:matching-distance}
 d(\mathbf z,\pi)=
 \min\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}L\bigl((z_s\otimes\bar\pi(s))_{s\in S}\bigr),$$ where the spectrum is taken in $C\otimes\mathcal B(\bar V_\pi)$. Matrix algebras have a unique $C^*$-tensor norm. On any faithful unital Hilbert-space realization $C\subseteq
\mathcal B(H)$, the number in <a href="#eq:matching-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matching-distance">[eq:matching-distance]</a> equals $$\inf_{\substack{\xi\in H\otimes\bar V_\pi\\\|\xi\|=1}}
 \sum_{s\in S}\|(z_s\otimes\bar\pi(s))\xi-\xi\|^2.$$ In such a faithful realization, if $z_s=\rho(s)$ comes from a unitary representation and $\pi$ is irreducible, then $d(\mathbf z,\pi)=0$ exactly when $\pi$ occurs in $\rho$, and otherwise $d(\mathbf z,\pi)\ge\kappa$. Indeed, invariant vectors of $\rho\otimes\bar\pi$ correspond to intertwiners from $V_\pi$ to $H$, and the spectral gap separates their orthogonal complement from zero.

<a id="eq:block-minimum"></a>
The number $d(\mathbf z,\pi)$ is unchanged by unitary equivalence of $\pi$. For a finite direct sum, the same formula gives $$\label{eq:block-minimum}
 d(\mathbf z,\pi_1\oplus\cdots\oplus\pi_r)
   =\min_{1\le i\le r}d(\mathbf z,\pi_i).$$ Fix once and for all <a id="eq:thresholds"></a>$$\label{eq:thresholds}
 b=\frac{\kappa^2}{4|S|},\qquad 0<a<\min\{\kappa,b\}.$$ The next section proves that, for a tuple in a nuclear algebra, only finitely many irreducible $\pi$ can satisfy $d(\mathbf z,\pi)<a$. No group relations on that tuple will be needed.

<a id="sec:nuclear"></a>
### Finiteness of approximate representation matches

We prove that, for a fixed unitary tuple $\mathbf u$ in a nuclear algebra, only finitely many finite-dimensional irreducible representation classes satisfy $d(\mathbf u,\pi)<a$. The tuple need not satisfy any group relations. This distinction will allow us to apply the result to coordinate lifts from a norm ultrapower.

We retain the notation and constants of Section <a href="#sec:spectral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spectral">3</a>. For $C^*$-algebras $B$ and $C$, the minimal tensor product $B\otimes_{\min}C$ is realized by faithful representations on separate Hilbert spaces; the maximal tensor product $B\otimes_{\max}C$ is characterized by pairs of commuting representations on one Hilbert space. We use the tensor-norm characterization of nuclearity developed by Takesaki and Lance (Takesaki 1964; Lance 1973) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Takesaki1964](#ref-Takesaki1964), [Lance1973](#ref-Lance1973)]: $B$ is nuclear if these two tensor norms agree for every $C$. We first obtain a lower bound when the second tensor factor has no finite-dimensional subrepresentations. The passage from a vector to its reduced density operator removes the arbitrary unitaries in the first factor.

<a id="stmt-lem-square-root-trace"></a>
<div id="lem:square-root-trace" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Square-root trace inequality). *For positive Hilbert–Schmidt operators $X,Y$ on any Hilbert space, $$\|X-Y\|_2^2\leq\|X^2-Y^2\|_1.$$ Here $\|\cdot\|_1$ and $\|\cdot\|_2$ denote the trace and Hilbert–Schmidt norms, respectively.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-square-root-trace"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-square-root-trace</summary>

*Proof.* This is the square-root trace inequality of Powers–Størmer (Powers and Størmer 1970, Lemma 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[PowersStormer1970](#ref-PowersStormer1970)]; we include the short argument needed here. Put $F=X-Y$, and let $J=\operatorname{sgn}(F)$, with value zero on $\ker F$. Since $X^2-Y^2=FX+YF$ and $JF=FJ=|F|$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \|X^2-Y^2\|_1
 &\geq \operatorname{Tr}\bigl(J(X^2-Y^2)\bigr)\\
 &=\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(|F|(X+Y)\bigr)\\
 &\geq\operatorname{Tr}(F_+F)+\operatorname{Tr}(F_-(-F))
 =\operatorname{Tr}(F^2).
\end{aligned}$$ For the last inequality, use $X+Y-F=2Y\geq0$ and $X+Y+F=2X\geq0$, and pair these inequalities with $F_+$ and $F_-$, respectively.

All these traces are well defined in infinite dimension. Products of two Hilbert–Schmidt operators are trace class; their traces are cyclic, by finite-rank approximation and $\|AB\|_1\leq\|A\|_2\|B\|_2$. The trace of a product of two positive Hilbert–Schmidt operators is nonnegative, as is seen by diagonalizing one factor. These observations also justify the cyclic rearrangement with the bounded operator $J$. The operators involved vanish on the orthogonal complement of a separable subspace, so no separability hypothesis on the ambient Hilbert space is needed. ◻

</details>

Bekka (Bekka 1990) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Bekka1990](#ref-Bekka1990)] proved that if a unitary representation of a property (T) group on a Hilbert space $K$ admits a state on $\mathcal B(K)$ invariant under conjugation by the representation, then it has a nonzero finite-dimensional subrepresentation; see also (Valette 1997, proof of Proposition 2) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Valette1997](#ref-Valette1997)]. The next lemma gives the explicit spectral bound needed here for an arbitrary unitary tuple in the first tensor factor.

<a id="stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound"></a>
<div id="lem:weak-mixing-bound" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Uniform spectral bound). *Let $\Gamma,S,\kappa$ satisfy <a href="#eq:kazhdan-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kazhdan-gap">[eq:kazhdan-gap]</a>. Let $\sigma:\Gamma\to\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation with no nonzero finite-dimensional subrepresentation. For any Hilbert space $E$ and any unitaries $(U_s)_{s\in S}$ on $E$, $$L\bigl((U_s\otimes\sigma(s))_{s\in S}\bigr)\geq b1,
 \qquad b=\frac{\kappa^2}{4|S|}.$$ No relations among the $U_s$ are required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weak-mixing-bound</summary>

*Proof.* Conjugation by $\sigma$ is a unitary representation on the Hilbert space $\mathcal S_2(K)$ of Hilbert–Schmidt operators. It has no nonzero invariant vector: if $A\neq0$ commutes with every $\sigma(g)$, then so does the nonzero positive compact operator $|A|$. The eigenspace of any nonzero eigenvalue of $|A|$ is a nonzero finite-dimensional $\sigma$-invariant subspace.

Use inner products linear in the first variable. Fix a unit vector $\xi\in E\otimes K$. Its reduced density operator $T$ on $K$ is characterized by $$\operatorname{Tr}(TC)=\langle(1\otimes C)\xi,\xi\rangle
 \qquad(C\in\mathcal B(K)).$$ Concretely, write $\xi=\sum_i e_i\otimes v_i$ with $(e_i)$ orthonormal and $\sum_i\|v_i\|^2=1$. Then $T=\sum_i |v_i\rangle\langle v_i|$, with convergence in trace norm; in particular, $T\geq0$ and $\operatorname{Tr}(T)=1$. Such an expansion uses only countably many vectors, even if the Hilbert spaces are nonseparable.

For $s\in S$, put $\xi_s=(U_s\otimes\sigma(s))\xi$ and $\delta_s=\|\xi_s-\xi\|$. The reduced density operator of $\xi_s$ is $T_s=\sigma(s)T\sigma(s)^*$. Testing the two vector states against $1\otimes C$, for $\|C\|\leq1$, gives $$\|T_s-T\|_1\leq2\delta_s.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:square-root-trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:square-root-trace">4</a> therefore gives $$\|\sigma(s)T^{1/2}\sigma(s)^*-T^{1/2}\|_2^2
 \leq\|T_s-T\|_1\leq2\delta_s.$$ Since $\|T^{1/2}\|_2=1$, applying <a href="#eq:kazhdan-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kazhdan-gap">[eq:kazhdan-gap]</a> to the conjugation representation and then Cauchy–Schwarz yields $$\kappa\leq2\sum_{s\in S}\delta_s
 \leq2\sqrt{|S|}\left(\sum_{s\in S}\delta_s^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ Thus $\sum_s\delta_s^2\geq b$. Taking all unit vectors $\xi$ proves the operator inequality; when $E\otimes K=\{0\}$ it is vacuous. ◻

</details>

The following quotient argument uses Wassermann’s property (T) method (Wassermann 1991) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Wassermann1991](#ref-Wassermann1991)]; see the exposition in (Manuilov and Thomsen 2007, sec. 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[ManuilovThomsen2007](#ref-ManuilovThomsen2007)]. The preceding uniform bound allows us to use an arbitrary unitary tuple in the first factor. Nuclearity will transfer the bound to the coordinate quotient and contradict the assumed small spectral minima.

<a id="stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness"></a>
<div id="lem:nuclear-finiteness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Nuclear finiteness). *Let $\Gamma,S,\kappa,R,b,a$ be as above. For every nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and every unitary $S$-tuple $\mathbf u$ in $B$, the set $$D(\mathbf u)=\{[\pi]:d(\mathbf u,\pi)<a\}$$ is finite, where $\pi$ ranges over the nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations of $\Gamma$ and $[\pi]$ denotes its unitary equivalence class. No group relations on $\mathbf u$ are assumed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-nuclear-finiteness</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $(\pi_j)_{j\geq1}$ are pairwise inequivalent representations with $[\pi_j]\in D(\mathbf u)$. Write $M_j=\mathcal B(\bar V_{\pi_j})$, and form $$Q=\frac{\prod_{j\geq1}M_j}{\bigoplus_{j\geq1}M_j},
 \qquad q(g)=[(\bar\pi_j(g))_j]\quad(g\in\Gamma).$$ The product consists of bounded sequences, and the direct sum is the ideal of sequences whose norms tend to zero in the ordinary sense. In particular, this auxiliary quotient does not involve an ultrafilter.

<a id="eq:quotient-excludes-finite-parts"></a>
Choose a faithful unital representation $\eta:Q\to\mathcal B(K)$, and put $\sigma=\eta\circ q$. We first show that $\sigma$ has no nonzero finite-dimensional subrepresentations. Fix a finite-dimensional irreducible representation $\tau$ of $\Gamma$. Under the identification $$\bar V_{\pi_j}\otimes\bar V_\tau
 \simeq\mathcal S_2(V_\tau,\bar V_{\pi_j}),$$ invariant vectors for $\bar\pi_j\otimes\bar\tau$ correspond to intertwiners from $\tau$ to $\bar\pi_j$. By irreducibility and inequivalence, such an intertwiner can exist for at most one $j$. Consequently, $$L\bigl((\bar\pi_j(s)\otimes\bar\tau(s))_{s\in S}\bigr)
 \geq\kappa1$$ outside at most one coordinate. Fixed-size matrix amplification commutes with this quotient, entry by entry: $$Q\otimes\mathcal B(\bar V_\tau)
 \simeq
 \frac{\prod_j(M_j\otimes\mathcal B(\bar V_\tau))}
 {\bigoplus_j(M_j\otimes\mathcal B(\bar V_\tau))}.$$ The exceptional coordinate disappears, and hence $$\label{eq:quotient-excludes-finite-parts}
 L\bigl((q(s)\otimes\bar\tau(s))_{s\in S}\bigr)\geq\kappa1.$$ If $\sigma$ contained $\tau$, then $\sigma\otimes\bar\tau$ would have an invariant vector, contradicting <a href="#eq:quotient-excludes-finite-parts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quotient-excludes-finite-parts">[eq:quotient-excludes-finite-parts]</a>. Every nonzero finite-dimensional unitary representation contains an irreducible subrepresentation, so $\sigma$ has none.

<a id="eq:spatial-lower-bound"></a>
Represent $B$ faithfully and unitally on a Hilbert space $E$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:weak-mixing-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weak-mixing-bound">5</a>, $$\label{eq:spatial-lower-bound}
 L\bigl((u_s\otimes q(s))_{s\in S}\bigr)\geq b1
 \quad\text{in }B\otimes_{\min}Q.$$ We now use nuclearity to pass this inequality to the coordinate quotient. Let $$C=\frac{\prod_j(B\otimes M_j)}{\bigoplus_j(B\otimes M_j)}.$$ There are commuting unital $*$-homomorphisms $$\alpha:B\longrightarrow C,\quad
 \alpha(x)=[(x\otimes1_{M_j})_j],
 \qquad
 \beta:Q\longrightarrow C,\quad
 \beta([(y_j)_j])=[(1_B\otimes y_j)_j].$$ The map $\beta$ is well defined because $\|1_B\otimes y_j\|=\|y_j\|$. The universal property of the maximal tensor product gives a unital $*$-homomorphism $$\Psi:B\otimes_{\max}Q\longrightarrow C,
 \qquad \Psi(x\otimes q)=\alpha(x)\beta(q).$$ Since $B$ is nuclear, $B\otimes_{\min}Q=B\otimes_{\max}Q$, so <a href="#eq:spatial-lower-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spatial-lower-bound">[eq:spatial-lower-bound]</a> passes through $\Psi$.

Put $L_j=L((u_s\otimes\bar\pi_j(s))_{s\in S})$. We have proved $[(L_j)_j]\geq b1$ in $C$. Continuous functional calculus for $f(t)=(b-t)_+$ now gives $$[(f(L_j))_j]=f([(L_j)_j])=0,
 \qquad
 \|f(L_j)\|=(b-d(\mathbf u,\pi_j))_+\longrightarrow0.$$ Thus $\liminf_j d(\mathbf u,\pi_j)\geq b$, whereas every term is less than $a<b$. This contradiction proves finiteness. ◻

</details>

The only use of nuclearity was to identify the minimal and maximal tensor products before applying $\Psi$. In particular, the proof does not assume an identification of the minimal tensor product with the coordinate quotient, and it imposes no bound on the dimensions of the $\pi_j$.

<a id="sec:ultrapower-contradiction"></a>
### From an ultrapower embedding to a finite contradiction

<span id="sec:contradiction" label="sec:contradiction"></span>

To prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, fix a nonzero unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra $B$ and a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. A set of indices is called $\omega$-large if it belongs to $\omega$. Suppose, towards a contradiction, that there is a unital injective $*$-homomorphism $$\Phi:C^*(G)\longrightarrow B^\omega.$$ We will associate a finite set of irreducible representations of $\Gamma$ to each coordinate of $\Phi$. Conjugation by $t$ will force these sets to contain predecessors under restriction by $\theta$. The resulting finite chains contradict the choice of $\pi_*$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Unitary lifts and a gap for varying matrix dimensions

Write $u_g$ for the canonical unitary of $g\in G$ in $C^*(G)$. Each unitary in $B^\omega$ has a representative consisting of unitaries in $B$. Indeed, if $(y_n)$ represents a unitary, then $$\lim_\omega\|y_n^*y_n-1\|
 =\lim_\omega\|y_ny_n^*-1\|=0.$$ On an $\omega$-large set both norms are less than $1/2$, so $y_n$ is invertible and $y_n(y_n^*y_n)^{-1/2}$ is unitary. The difference between this unitary and $y_n$ tends to zero along $\omega$, by continuous functional calculus. Set the representative equal to $1$ outside this set.

<a id="eq:lift-defects"></a>
Choose such representatives $x_n(g)\in\mathcal U(B)$ of $\Phi(u_g)$ for every $g\in G$, with $x_n(e)=1$. For each fixed pair $g,h\in G$, $$\lim_\omega\|x_n(g)x_n(h)-x_n(gh)\|=0.
 \label{eq:lift-defects}$$ These choices impose no group relations at any individual coordinate. All uses of <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a> below involve finitely many fixed group elements; no intersection over all pairs is needed. Put $\mathbf x_n=(x_n(s))_{s\in S}$. Among the unitary equivalence classes of nonzero finite-dimensional irreducible representations of $\Gamma$, define <a id="eq:coordinate-D"></a>$$D_n=\{[\pi]:d(\mathbf x_n,\pi)<a\}.
 \label{eq:coordinate-D}$$ Here brackets denote unitary equivalence classes. Each $D_n$ is finite by Lemma <a href="#lem:nuclear-finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:nuclear-finiteness">6</a>, which applies to arbitrary unitary $S$-tuples.

The next observation provides uniform control even when the dimensions of the representations increase with $n$.

<a id="stmt-lem-varying-gap"></a>
<div id="lem:varying-gap" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:varying-gap"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *For every sequence $(\pi_n)$ of nonzero finite-dimensional unitary representations of $\Gamma$, with no bound on their dimensions, $$\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)\in\{0\}\cup[\kappa,R].
 \label{eq:varying-gap}$$ In particular, if $\{n:d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)<a\}\in\omega$, then this limit is zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-varying-gap"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-varying-gap</summary>

<a id="eq:ultraproduct-norm"></a>
*Proof.* Let $C_n=B\otimes\mathcal B(\overline{V_{\pi_n}})$, and form the norm ultraproduct $$C_\omega=\prod_n C_n\big/
 \{(c_n):\lim_\omega\|c_n\|=0\}.$$ The product consists of bounded sequences. For every such sequence, $$\|[(c_n)]\|=\lim_\omega\|c_n\|.
 \label{eq:ultraproduct-norm}$$ For the lower bound, adding an $\omega$-null sequence does not change the ultralimit of the norms. For the upper bound, if this ultralimit is $c$, replace $c_n$ by $0$ outside $\{n:\|c_n\|<c+\varepsilon\}\in\omega$ and then let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$.

Equation <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a> shows that $$g\longmapsto[(x_n(g)\otimes\overline{\pi_n}(g))_n],
 \qquad g\in\Gamma,$$ is a genuine unitary representation in the nonzero unital algebra $C_\omega$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:spectral-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:spectral-gap">3</a>, its Laplacian has spectrum in $\{0\}\cup[\kappa,R]$. Write $$P_n=L\big((x_n(s)\otimes\overline{\pi_n}(s))_{s\in S}\big).$$ Since $0\leq P_n\leq R1$, equation <a href="#eq:ultraproduct-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ultraproduct-norm">[eq:ultraproduct-norm]</a> gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \min\operatorname{spec}[(P_n)]
 &=R-\|R1-[(P_n)]\|\\
 &=\lim_\omega\bigl(R-\|R1-P_n\|\bigr)
 =\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n).
\end{aligned}$$ This proves <a href="#eq:varying-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:varying-gap">[eq:varying-gap]</a>. In the final assertion the limit is at most $a<\kappa$, and hence must be zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The initial representation occurs

We next use injectivity of $\Phi$ to ensure that $[\pi_*]\in D_n$ for an $\omega$-large set of indices.

<a id="stmt-lem-initial-match"></a>
<div id="lem:initial-match" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8**. *We have $\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_*)=0$, and consequently $\{n:[\pi_*]\in D_n\}\in\omega$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-initial-match"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-initial-match</summary>

*Proof.* Induce $\pi_*$ from $\Gamma$ to $G$ on the Hilbert direct sum of copies of $V_{\pi_*}$ indexed by the left cosets $G/\Gamma$. Explicitly, choose representatives $q_i$, including the identity: when $gq_i=q_j\gamma$ with $\gamma\in\Gamma$, the action sends coordinate $i$ to coordinate $j$ by $\pi_*(\gamma)$. This defines a unitary representation. The identity coordinate is invariant under $\Gamma$ and carries $\pi_*$. Thus the restriction of this representation of $G$ contains $\pi_*$. Tensoring with $\overline{\pi_*}$ gives a nonzero $\Gamma$-invariant vector: on this coordinate it is $\sum_j e_j\otimes\overline{e_j}$ for an orthonormal basis $(e_j)$ of $V_{\pi_*}$.

Every unitary representation of $G$ gives a representation of the full group algebra $C^*(G)$. The preceding invariant vector shows that $$\min\operatorname{spec}
 L\big((u_s\otimes\overline{\pi_*}(s))_{s\in S}\big)=0.$$ Indeed, an invertible positive element could not acquire a kernel under a unital representation. The matrix amplification of $\Phi$ is injective and preserves spectra, so the corresponding spectral minimum in $B^\omega\otimes\mathcal B(\overline{V_{\pi_*}})$ is also zero.

For each fixed positive integer $d$ there is the canonical identification $$M_d(B^\omega)\cong (M_d(B))^\omega.$$ To check its kernel, a sequence of $d$-by-$d$ matrices tends to zero in norm along $\omega$ precisely when each of its finitely many entries does. Entrywise lifts give surjectivity. Applying this identification with $d=\dim\pi_*$ and the calculation of spectral minima in Lemma <a href="#lem:varying-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:varying-gap">7</a> proves the assertion. This fixed matrix amplification is distinct from the varying algebras used in that lemma. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### A predecessor for every representation at one coordinate

For representations $\alpha$ and $\beta$ of the same group, write $\alpha\prec\beta$ when $\alpha$ is unitarily equivalent to a subrepresentation of $\beta$. The required quantifier order is the following: on one $\omega$-large set, every member of $D_n$ has a predecessor in that same finite set.

<a id="stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor"></a>
<div id="lem:uniform-predecessor" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *The set $$\{n:(\forall[\pi]\in D_n)(\exists[\pi']\in D_n)
 \quad\pi\prec\pi'\circ\theta\}$$ belongs to $\omega$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-uniform-predecessor</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose otherwise. On an $\omega$-large set $F$, choose an irreducible $\pi_n$ with $[\pi_n]\in D_n$ for which no such predecessor exists. Outside $F$, choose the trivial representation. By Lemma <a href="#lem:varying-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:varying-gap">7</a>, <a id="eq:chosen-zero"></a>$$\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)=0.
 \label{eq:chosen-zero}$$

Set $\alpha_n=\pi_n\circ\theta^{-1}$, a representation of $\Lambda$, and let $\rho_n=\operatorname{Ind}_{\Lambda}^{\Gamma}\alpha_n$. We will prove that $\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\rho_n)=0$ and that every irreducible constituent $\pi'$ of $\rho_n$ satisfies $\pi_n\prec\pi'\circ\theta$. The block-minimum identity <a href="#eq:block-minimum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:block-minimum">[eq:block-minimum]</a> will then select a constituent belonging to $D_n$ on an $\omega$-large set, and the second assertion will make that constituent a predecessor of $\pi_n$.

<a id="eq:fixed-word-invariance"></a>
*Transport to $\Lambda$.* Represent $B$ faithfully and unitally on a Hilbert space $\mathcal H$, and use the same notation for its unitaries on $\mathcal H$. Choose unit vectors $\xi_n\in\mathcal H\otimes\overline{V_{\pi_n}}$ such that $$\sum_{s\in S}
 \|(x_n(s)\otimes\overline{\pi_n}(s))\xi_n-\xi_n\|^2
 <d(\mathbf x_n,\pi_n)+1/n.$$ Because $\omega$ is free, $1/n$ tends to zero along $\omega$. Thus $$\lim_\omega
 \|(x_n(g)\otimes\overline{\pi_n}(g))\xi_n-\xi_n\|=0
 \qquad(g\in\Gamma).
 \label{eq:fixed-word-invariance}$$ Here is the fixed-word justification. Put $U_n(g)=x_n(g)\otimes\overline{\pi_n}(g)$. For each $s\in S$, $x_n(s^{-1})-x_n(s)^*$ tends to zero by <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a>; a unitary and its inverse have the same displacement norm on a vector. For a fixed word $g=s_1^{\varepsilon_1}\cdots s_k^{\varepsilon_k}$, repeated use of <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a> replaces $U_n(g)$ by the corresponding product of $U_n(s_j)^{\varepsilon_j}$ with an error tending to zero. The displacement of that product is at most the sum of the $k$ displacements of its factors. Both the word and $k$ are fixed while $n$ varies.

<a id="eq:Lambda-invariance"></a>
Put $\eta_n=(x_n(t)\otimes1)\xi_n$. These are unit vectors. For $h\in\Lambda$ put $g=\theta^{-1}(h)$. The identity $ht=tg$ and <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a> give $$\lim_\omega\|x_n(h)x_n(t)-x_n(t)x_n(g)\|=0.$$ Together with <a href="#eq:fixed-word-invariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fixed-word-invariance">[eq:fixed-word-invariance]</a>, this proves $$\lim_\omega
 \|(x_n(h)\otimes\overline{\alpha_n}(h))\eta_n-\eta_n\|=0
 \qquad(h\in\Lambda).
 \label{eq:Lambda-invariance}$$

*Induction to $\Gamma$.* We transfer these vectors from $\Lambda$ to $\Gamma$ by a fixed eight-coordinate induction. Use the left coset representatives $r_1=e,r_2,\ldots,r_8$ of $\Gamma/\Lambda$ given by the translations in $\{0,1\}^3$. For $g\in\Gamma$ and $1\leq i\leq8$, define $j(g,i)$ and $h(g,i)\in\Lambda$ uniquely by $$gr_i=r_{j(g,i)}h(g,i).$$ The induced representation $\rho_n$ acts on $\bigoplus_{i=1}^8 V_{\pi_n}$: $\rho_n(g)$ sends coordinate $i$ to coordinate $j(g,i)$ by $\alpha_n(h(g,i))$. The relation $$h(g_1g_2,i)=h(g_1,j(g_2,i))h(g_2,i)$$ verifies the representation law. Permutation of the coordinates and unitarity of $\alpha_n$ verify unitarity. Its conjugate has the same coordinate rule with $\overline{\alpha_n}$ in place of $\alpha_n$.

Define $\zeta_n\in\mathcal H\otimes\overline{V_{\rho_n}}$ by $$(\zeta_n)_i=8^{-1/2}(x_n(r_i)\otimes1)\eta_n.$$ The eight summands are orthogonal and each has norm $8^{-1/2}$, so $\|\zeta_n\|=1$. Fix $s\in S$ and $i$, and write $j=j(s,i)$ and $h=h(s,i)$. The component sent from $i$ to $j$ by $x_n(s)\otimes\overline{\rho_n}(s)$ differs from $(\zeta_n)_j$ by a vector whose norm is at most $$\begin{aligned}
 8^{-1/2}\big(&\|x_n(s)x_n(r_i)-x_n(r_j)x_n(h)\|\\
 &+\|(x_n(h)\otimes\overline{\alpha_n}(h))\eta_n-\eta_n\|\big).
\end{aligned}$$ The first term tends to zero by <a href="#eq:lift-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-defects">[eq:lift-defects]</a> and $sr_i=r_jh$; the second does so by <a href="#eq:Lambda-invariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:Lambda-invariance">[eq:Lambda-invariance]</a>. There are only $8|S|$ fixed choices involved. Hence $$\lim_\omega\sum_{s\in S}
 \|(x_n(s)\otimes\overline{\rho_n}(s))\zeta_n-\zeta_n\|^2=0.$$ Thus $\lim_\omega d(\mathbf x_n,\rho_n)=0$, and in particular $d(\mathbf x_n,\rho_n)<a$ on an $\omega$-large set. All error bounds are independent of $\dim\pi_n$.

*Selecting a predecessor.* Decompose the finite-dimensional unitary representation $\rho_n$ into irreducible summands. The corresponding Laplacian is a direct sum, so its spectral minimum is the minimum of the spectral minima of these summands. Consequently, on an $\omega$-large set, some irreducible constituent $\pi'_n$ of $\rho_n$ has $[\pi'_n]\in D_n$.

Every irreducible constituent $\pi'$ of $\rho_n$, including the one just obtained, satisfies $\pi_n\prec\pi'\circ\theta$. This is the finite-index form of Frobenius reciprocity. To verify it for every constituent, let $W$ be its invariant subspace and let $P_1:W\to V_{\pi_n}$ be projection onto the identity coordinate. This map is $\Lambda$-equivariant for the target representation $\alpha_n$. It is nonzero: if $w\in W$ has a nonzero $i$th coordinate, then $$P_1\rho_n(r_i^{-1})w=w_i\ne0.$$ Since $\alpha_n$ is irreducible, $P_1|_W$ is surjective. Its adjoint is an injective $\Lambda$-intertwiner; after normalization by a positive scalar it is an isometric intertwiner, because $(P_1|_W)(P_1|_W)^*$ commutes with the irreducible $\alpha_n$. It follows that $\alpha_n\prec\pi'|_\Lambda$; composing with $\theta:\Gamma\to\Lambda$ gives the asserted containment.

Intersect the $\omega$-large set on which such a $[\pi'_n]$ belongs to $D_n$ with $F$. This contradicts the choice of $\pi_n$ and proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Finite chains force periodicity

The analytic argument is complete. We finish with the elementary consequence of finiteness that makes the predecessor property incompatible with $\pi_*$. Consider a chain of finite-dimensional irreducible unitary representations $\tau_0=\pi,\tau_1,\ldots$ of $\Gamma$ whose classes lie in a finite set $D$ and satisfy $\tau_i\prec\tau_{i+1}\circ\theta$. Finiteness gives indices $l\geq0$ and $p\geq1$ with $\tau_l\simeq\tau_{l+p}$. Figure <a href="#fig:finite-chain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:finite-chain">1</a> separates the initial segment of this chain from the repeated segment. The key point is that periodicity at the repeated class forces every pullback of that representation to remain irreducible, so that periodicity reaches the starting class.

<figure id="fig:finite-chain">

<figcaption>A chain inside one finite set $D$. An arrow from $[τ_{i}]$ to $[τ_{i + 1}]$ means $τ_{i} ≺ τ_{i + 1} ∘ θ$. Repetition gives $τ_{l} ≃ τ_{l} ∘ θ^{p}$ by equality of dimensions. The proof then shows that each pullback of $τ_{l}$ is irreducible, allowing this periodicity to pass back to $π$. The initial segment can be empty.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-finite-chain"></a>

```latex
\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth, every node/.style={font=\small}]
  \node (start) at (0,0) {$[\tau_0]=[\pi]$};
  \node (tail) at (2,0) {$\cdots$};
  \node (cycle) at (3.7,0) {$[\tau_l]$};
  \node (middle) at (5.4,0) {$\cdots$};
  \node (repeat) at (7.4,0) {$[\tau_{l+p}]$};
  \draw[->] (start) -- (tail);
  \draw[->] (tail) -- (cycle);
  \draw[->] (cycle) -- (middle);
  \draw[->] (middle) -- (repeat);
  \draw[dashed] (cycle.north) to[out=55,in=125]
     node[above] {same equivalence class} (repeat.north);
  \node at (1.7,-0.65) {initial segment};

\end{tikzpicture}
```
